\displaystyle\left.\frac{2}{\beta}\frac{{\partial}}{{\partial}h^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q},\beta}{\partial}h^{\mu}_{-\textbf{q},\nu}}\left(\sum_{\lambda=1}^{N_{\lambda}}\frac{(\tilde{\textbf{N}}_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}}]^{T})^{\lambda}\tilde{\textbf{N}}_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{-\textbf{q}}]^{\lambda}}{\omega_{\textbf{q},\lambda}}\right)\right|_{\textbf{h}=0}\;.

(313)

The dynamical factor associated with the operator
\hat{O}
is defined by

\displaystyle S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{O}}(\textbf{q})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha,\beta}{\langle\hat{O}^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q},\beta}\hat{O}^{\beta}_{-\textbf{q},\alpha}\rangle}

(314)

We can generalize a spectral decomposition of the structure factors as

\displaystyle S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{O}}(\textbf{q},\omega)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha,\beta,\lambda}{\langle\hat{O}^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q},\beta}\hat{O}^{\beta}_{-\textbf{q},\alpha}\rangle}_{\lambda}\delta(\omega-\omega_{\textbf{q},\lambda})\;,

(315)

and calculate the following quantity

\displaystyle S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{O}}(\textbf{q},\omega)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha,\beta,\lambda}\left[{\langle\hat{O}^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q},\beta}\rangle}_{\lambda}{\langle\hat{O}^{\beta}_{-\textbf{q},\alpha}\rangle}_{\lambda}+\chi^{\alpha\beta\beta\alpha}_{\lambda}(\textbf{q})\right]

(316)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\delta(\omega-\omega_{\textbf{q},\lambda})\;,

where generalized susceptibility

\displaystyle\chi^{\alpha\beta\mu\nu}_{\lambda}(\textbf{q})={\langle\hat{O}^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q},\beta}\hat{O}^{\mu}_{-\textbf{q},\nu}\rangle}_{\lambda}-{\langle\hat{O}^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q},\beta}\rangle}_{\lambda}{\langle\hat{O}^{\mu}_{-\textbf{q},\nu}\rangle}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\left.\frac{2}{\beta}\frac{{\partial}}{{\partial}h^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q},\beta}{\partial}h^{\mu}_{-\textbf{q},\nu}}\left(\frac{(\tilde{\textbf{N}}_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}}]^{T})^{\lambda}\tilde{\textbf{N}}_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{-\textbf{q}}]^{\lambda}}{\omega_{\textbf{q},\lambda}}\right)\right|_{\textbf{h}=0}\;.

(317)

is diagonal in
\lambda
.
From
Eq. (306)
, we note that the first moments
{\langle\hat{O}^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q},\beta}\rangle}
will only contribute at
\textbf{q}=0
. Therefore, for
\textbf{q}\neq 0
, we can neglect the
{\langle\hat{O}^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q},\lambda}\rangle}{\langle\hat{O}^{\beta}_{-\textbf{q},\lambda}\rangle}
term and we obtain

\displaystyle S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{O}}(\textbf{q}\neq 0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha\beta\lambda}\chi^{\alpha\beta\beta\alpha}_{\lambda}(\textbf{q})+\mathcal{O}(T^{2})\;,

(318)

D.1
Structure factors classically at
\textbf{q}=0

We here show how the calculation for the structure at
\textbf{q}=0
is obtained.
At
\textbf{q}=0
, the structure factor associated with the operator
\hat{O}
is defined by

\displaystyle S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{O}}(\textbf{q}=0)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha,\beta}{\langle\hat{O}^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}=0,\beta}\hat{O}^{\beta}_{\textbf{q}=0,\alpha}\rangle}\;.

(319)

The relevant source term is given by
Eq. (154)
.
By expanding
Eq. (154)
in terms of the fluctuations, we obtained
Eq. (300)
. We see that the contribution of the second order in fluctuations has the same form as the Hamiltonian expressed as
Eq. (124)
and will enter the interaction matrix
M_{\textbf{k}}
, modifying its eigenvalues, i.e., relation dispersion relations, which will all also depend on the field
h
.
We can therefore assume that, at
\textbf{q}=0
, the total Hamiltonian [
Eq. (153)
] has the following form

\displaystyle{\cal H}

\displaystyle=E_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\textbf{k}}\left[\vec{\phi}_{\textbf{k}}^{T}M_{\textbf{k}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]\vec{\phi}_{-\textbf{k}}\right]

\displaystyle+\sum_{\textbf{k}}\left[N^{T}_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]\vec{\phi}_{-\textbf{k}}+\vec{\phi}_{\textbf{k}}^{T}N_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{-\textbf{k}}]\right]\delta_{\textbf{k},0}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\textbf{k}}C[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]\delta_{\textbf{k},0}+\mathcal{O}(\phi^{3})\;,

(320)

where
C[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]
represents the 0–order term in
\phi_{\textbf{q}}
of the moments
\hat{O}_{\textbf{q}}
. The 2
nd
–order contribution at
\textbf{q}=0
is now included in
M_{\textbf{k}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]
. As before,
\textbf{N}_{1,2}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]
are n–dimensional vectors whose components depend linearly on the fields
h^{\alpha}_{\textbf{k},\beta}
and represent the linear terms in
\phi_{\textbf{k}}
of the moments
\hat{O}_{\textbf{k}}
.
Neglecting terms in
{\mathcal{O}}(\phi^{3})
, and using
Eq. (494e)
to perform the integral, we find

\displaystyle Z=

\displaystyle e^{-\beta E_{0}}\prod_{\textbf{k}}^{N}\left[\int e^{-\beta\frac{1}{2}\vec{\phi}_{\textbf{k}}^{T}M_{\textbf{k}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]\vec{\phi}_{\textbf{k}}}\right.

\displaystyle\times e^{-\beta\left[N^{T}_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]\vec{\phi}_{-\textbf{k}}+\vec{\phi}_{\textbf{k}}^{T}N_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{-\textbf{k}}]\right]\delta_{\textbf{k},0}}

\displaystyle\left.\times e^{-\beta C[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]\delta_{\textbf{k},0}}\right]{\rm d}\vec{\phi}_{\textbf{k}}

(321a)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-\beta E_{0}}\prod_{\textbf{k}}^{N}\left[\sqrt{\frac{(2\pi)^{n}}{\beta^{n}\det M_{\textbf{k}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]}}\right.

\displaystyle\times\left.e^{2\beta N^{T}_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]M_{\textbf{k}}^{-1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]N_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{-\textbf{k}}]\delta_{\textbf{k},0}}e^{-\beta C[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]\delta_{\textbf{k},0}}\right]\;,

(321b)

where
E_{0}
is defined through
Eq. (125)
,
and the
n\times n
matrix
M_{\textbf{k}}[{h}^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}=0\beta}]
through
Eq. (320)
that includes up to second order in fluctuations.
n
is the dimension of
M_{\textbf{k}}[{h}^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}=0\beta}]
, i.e., the number of independent classical fluctuations. In our case, we have
n=4
.
N
is the number of lattice sites.
It follows that the free energy is

\displaystyle F=

\displaystyle-\frac{\log(Z)}{\beta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{0}+\sum_{\textbf{k}}C[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]\delta_{\textbf{k},0}

\displaystyle-\frac{n}{2\beta}\sum_{\textbf{k}}\log(\frac{(2\pi)}{\beta})+\frac{1}{2\beta}\sum_{\textbf{k}}\sum_{\lambda=1}^{N_{\lambda}}\log(\omega_{\textbf{k},\lambda}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}])

\displaystyle-2\sum_{\textbf{k}}N^{T}_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]M_{\textbf{k}}^{-1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]N_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{-\textbf{k}}]\delta_{\textbf{k},0}+{\mathcal{O}}(T^{2})\;,

(322)

where
\omega_{\textbf{k},\lambda}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]
are the eigenvalues of
M_{\textbf{k}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]
,
and we have used
Eq. (137)
.
The moments are given by

\displaystyle{\langle\hat{O}^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}=0,\beta}\rangle}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left.\frac{{\partial}F}{{\partial}h^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}=0,\beta}}\right|_{\textbf{h}=0}\;,

(323)

and

\displaystyle{\langle\hat{O}^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}=0,\beta}\hat{O}^{\mu}_{\textbf{q}=0,\nu}\rangle}=

\displaystyle{\langle\hat{O}^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}=0,\beta}\rangle}{\langle\hat{O}^{\mu}_{\textbf{q}=0,\nu}\rangle}

\displaystyle-\left.\frac{1}{\beta}\frac{{\partial}^{2}F}{{\partial}h^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}=0,\beta}{\partial}h^{\mu}_{\textbf{q}=0,\nu}}\right|_{\textbf{h}=0}\;.

(324)

Using
Eq. (322)
,
Eq. (323)
yields

\displaystyle{\langle\hat{O}^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}=0,\beta}\rangle}

\displaystyle=-\left.\frac{{\partial}C[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]}{{\partial}h^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}=0,\beta}}\right|_{\textbf{h}=0}

\displaystyle-\left.\frac{1}{2\beta}\sum_{\textbf{k}}\sum_{\lambda=1}^{N_{\lambda}}\frac{1}{\omega_{\textbf{k},\lambda}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]}\frac{{\partial}\omega_{\textbf{k},\lambda}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]}{{\partial}h^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}=0,\beta}}\right|_{\textbf{h}=0}

\displaystyle+\left.2\frac{{\partial}\left[N^{T}_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]M_{\textbf{q}=0}^{-1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]N_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]\right]}{{\partial}h^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}=0,\beta}}\right|_{\textbf{h}=0}

\displaystyle+{\mathcal{O}}(T^{2})\;,

(325)

where the last derivative turns out to be null when evaluated at
\textbf{h}=0
, for dipoles, quadrupoles and A-matrices.

Eq. (324)
becomes

\displaystyle{\langle\hat{O}^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}=0,\beta}\hat{O}^{\mu}_{\textbf{q}=0,\nu}\rangle}

\displaystyle={\langle\hat{O}^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}=0,\beta}\rangle}{\langle\hat{O}^{\mu}_{\textbf{q}=0,\nu}\rangle}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\beta}

\displaystyle\left.\frac{{\partial}^{2}C[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]}{{\partial}h^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}=0,\beta}{\partial}h^{\mu}_{\textbf{q}=0,\nu}}\right|_{\textbf{h}=0}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{\beta}

\displaystyle\left.\frac{{\partial}^{2}\left[N^{T}_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]M_{\textbf{q}=0}^{-1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]N_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]\right]}{{\partial}h^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}=0,\beta}{\partial}h^{\mu}_{\textbf{q}=0,\nu}}\right|_{\textbf{h}=0}

\displaystyle+{\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle(T^{2})\;,

(326)

where the terms including second derivatives of
C[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]
are zero, since
C[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]
is linear in
{h}^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}=0,\beta}
by definition.
Finally,
Eq. (319)
can be calculated by using
Eq. (325)
and
Eq. (326)
.
For each type of moments, dipole, quadrupole or A-matrix, the interaction matrix
M_{\textbf{k}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]
, the source terms
N^{T}_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}}]
and
N_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}}]
and the constant term will be different. They are given below.

D.2
Dipole moments: classical structure factor for
\textbf{q}\neq 0

First, we consider the structure factor for dipole moments of spin

\displaystyle S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{S}}(\textbf{q})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha}{\langle{\hat{\rm{S}}_{\textbf{q}}^{\alpha}}{\hat{\rm{S}}_{-\textbf{q}}^{\alpha}}\rangle}\;.

(327)

The relevant source term is

\displaystyle\Delta{\cal H}[{h}^{\alpha}_{i,\beta}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{i,\lambda}h_{i,\beta}^{\alpha}\delta_{\alpha\beta}{\hat{\rm{S}}_{i,\lambda}^{\beta}}\;.

(328)

According to
Eq. (50)
and using
Eq. (121)
, we can express the spin dipole components in function of the fluctuations.
Considering fluctuation terms up to 1
st
order and using
Eq. (145)
,
the spin dipole moments in terms of the fluctuations diagonalizing the BBQ Hamiltonian are given by

\displaystyle{\hat{\rm{S}}_{i}^{x}}

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{2}v_{4,i}\;,

\displaystyle{\hat{\rm{S}}_{i}^{y}}

\displaystyle\simeq 0\;,

(329)

\displaystyle{\hat{\rm{S}}_{i}^{z}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}v_{2,i}\;.

After performing a Fourier transform, the change in the Hamiltonian due to
\Delta{\cal H}[{h}^{\alpha}_{i,\beta}]
[
Eq. (328)
] yields

\displaystyle\Delta{\cal H}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}}]=-\sum_{\textbf{q}}

\displaystyle\left[\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}h^{z}_{\textbf{q}}v_{-\textbf{q},2}+\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}h^{z}_{-\textbf{q}}v_{\textbf{q},2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}h^{x}_{\textbf{q}}v_{-\textbf{q},4}-\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}h^{x}_{-\textbf{q}}v_{\textbf{q},4}\right]\;,

and according to
Eq. (310)
, we get

\tilde{C}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}}]=0\;,

(330)

where we neglected 2
nd
order terms in fluctuations, since they only contribute for
\textbf{q}=0
, and

\begin{matrix}\tilde{\textbf{N}}_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}}]^{T}=\begin{pmatrix}0,&\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}h^{z}_{\textbf{q}},&0,&-\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}h^{x}_{\textbf{q}}\end{pmatrix}\;,\\
\tilde{\textbf{N}}_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{-\textbf{q}}]=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}h^{z}_{-\textbf{q}}\\
0\\
-\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}h^{x}_{-\textbf{q}}\end{pmatrix}\;.\end{matrix}

(331)

According to
Eq. (306)
, the first moments are given by the first derivative of
\tilde{C}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}}]
[
Eq. (330)
] with respect to the fictive field
h
.
We get

\displaystyle{\langle S^{x}_{\textbf{q}}\rangle}

\displaystyle={\langle S^{z}_{\textbf{q}}\rangle}=0\;.

(332)

The total structure factor for the dipole moment is given by
Eq. (327)
,
and using
Eq. (317)
,
Eq. (318)
, and
Eq. (331)
, we obtain

S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{S}}(\textbf{q}\neq 0)=\frac{2}{\beta\omega_{\textbf{q},2}}+\frac{2}{\beta\omega_{\textbf{q},4}}+{\mathcal{O}}(T^{2})=\frac{4}{\beta\omega_{\textbf{q}}^{-}}+{\mathcal{O}}(T^{2})\;,

(333)

where we used
Eq. (140b)
.
Its spectral decomposition [
Eq. (316)
] becomes

S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{S}}(\textbf{q}\neq 0,\omega)=\frac{4}{\beta\omega_{\textbf{q}}^{-}}\delta(\omega-\omega_{\textbf{q}}^{-})+{\mathcal{O}}(T^{2})\;.

(334)

D.3
Dipole moments: classical structure factor at
\textbf{q}=0

We now consider the dipole structure factor at the origin of the reciprocal space called the
\Gamma
–point.
We consider the structure factor for the spin dipole moments

\displaystyle S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{S}}(\textbf{q}=0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha}{\langle{\hat{\rm{S}}_{\textbf{q}=0}^{\alpha}}{\hat{\rm{S}}_{\textbf{q}=0}^{\alpha}}\rangle}\;.

(335)

We follow the procedure depicted in
Appendix
D.1
. The relevant source term for dipole moments is given by
Eq. (328)
that we need to rewrite it in the same form as
Eq. (320)
. We use
Eq. (124)
–
Eq. (127)
for the BBQ Hamiltonian, as well as,
Eq. (121)
and
Eq. (50)
to express
Eq. (328)
up to second order in terms of the fluctuations. For the total Hamiltonian given in
Eq. (153)
, and written in the form of
Eq. (320)
, we obtain

\displaystyle M_{\textbf{k}}

\displaystyle[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}A_{\textbf{k}}&-B_{\textbf{k}}&0&\frac{i}{\sqrt{N}}h^{y}_{\textbf{q}=0,y}\\
-B_{\textbf{k}}&A_{\textbf{k}}&-\frac{i}{\sqrt{N}}h^{y}_{\textbf{q}=0,y}&0\\
0&\frac{i}{\sqrt{N}}h^{y}_{\textbf{q}=0,y}&A_{\textbf{k}}&-B_{\textbf{k}}\\
-\frac{i}{\sqrt{N}}h^{y}_{\textbf{q}=0,y}&0&-B_{\textbf{k}}&A_{\textbf{k}}\\
\end{pmatrix}\;,

(336)

\begin{matrix}\textbf{N}_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]^{T}=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}-h^{z}_{\textbf{k},z},&-h^{z}_{\textbf{k},z},&h^{x}_{\textbf{k},x},&h^{x}_{\textbf{k},x}\end{pmatrix}\;,\\
\textbf{N}_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{-\textbf{k}}]=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}-h^{z}_{-\textbf{k},z}\\
-h^{z}_{-\textbf{k},z}\\
h^{x}_{-\textbf{k},x}\\
h^{x}_{-\textbf{k},x}\end{pmatrix}\;,\end{matrix}

(337)

C[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]=0\;.

(338)

We diagonalize
Eq. (336)
to obtain the eigenmodes. We find

\displaystyle\omega_{\textbf{k}}^{+}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]

\displaystyle=\omega_{\textbf{k},1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]=\omega_{\textbf{k},3}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]

\displaystyle=A_{\textbf{k}}+\sqrt{\left(\frac{h^{y}_{0,y}}{\sqrt{N}}\right)^{2}+B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}}\;,

(339a)

\displaystyle\omega_{\textbf{k}}^{-}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]

\displaystyle=\omega_{\textbf{k},2}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]=\omega_{\textbf{k},4}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]

\displaystyle=A_{\textbf{k}}-\sqrt{\left(\frac{h^{y}_{0,y}}{\sqrt{N}}\right)^{2}+B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}}\;.

(339b)

We now can calculate the spin dipole moments through
Eq. (325)
, where we use
Eq. (338)
, and
Eq. (339)
, and where for the last term, we simply invert
Eq. (336)
and multiply by the vectors in
Eq. (337)
.
We obtain

\displaystyle{\langle S^{x}_{\textbf{q}=0}\rangle}

\displaystyle={\langle S^{y}_{\textbf{q}=0}\rangle}={\langle S^{z}_{\textbf{q}=0}\rangle}=0\;.

(340)

For the square dipole moments, we use
Eq. (326)
. We find

\displaystyle{\langle S^{x}_{\textbf{q}=0}S^{x}_{\textbf{q}=0}\rangle}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\beta}\frac{1}{A_{\textbf{q}=0}-B_{\textbf{q}=0}}=\frac{2}{\beta}\frac{1}{\omega_{0}^{-}}\;,

(341a)

\displaystyle{\langle S^{y}_{\textbf{q}=0}S^{y}_{\textbf{q}=0}\rangle}

\displaystyle=0\;,

(341b)

\displaystyle{\langle S^{z}_{\textbf{q}=0}S^{z}_{\textbf{q}=0}\rangle}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\beta}\frac{1}{A_{\textbf{q}=0}-B_{\textbf{q}=0}}=\frac{2}{\beta}\frac{1}{\omega_{0}^{-}}\;,

(341c)

where we used
Eq. (140)
.

Finally, we calculate the dipole structure factor at the
\Gamma
–point given by
Eq. (335)
. We get

\displaystyle S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{S}}(\textbf{q}=0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4}{\beta}\frac{1}{\omega^{-}_{0}}+{\mathcal{O}}(T^{2})\;.

(342)

Because the
\textbf{q}=0
contributions are coming from the ground state and happen for
\omega=0
, the spectral representation of
Eq. (342)
yields

\displaystyle S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{S}}(\textbf{q}=0,\omega)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4}{\beta}\frac{1}{\omega^{-}_{0}}\delta(\omega)+{\mathcal{O}}(T^{2})\;.

(343)

Combining
Eq. (333)
and
Eq. (342)
, we get
Eq. (157)
. And considering their respective spectral representation
Eq. (334)
and
Eq. (343)
, we obtain
Eq. (158)
.

D.4
Quadrupole moments: classical structure factor for
\textbf{q}\neq 0

Next, we consider the structure factor for quadrupole moments of spin

\displaystyle S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{Q}}(\textbf{q})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha\beta}{\langle{\hat{\rm{Q}}_{\textbf{q}}^{\alpha\beta}}{\hat{\rm{Q}}_{-\textbf{q}}^{\beta\alpha}}\rangle}\;,

(344)

where the scalar contraction implied by the sum on
\alpha
,
\beta
respects

SU(2)
symmetry.
In this case the source term is

\Delta{\cal H}[{\bf{h}}_{i}]=-\sum_{i}h^{\alpha}_{i,\beta}{\hat{\rm{Q}}_{i}^{\alpha\beta}}\;.

(345)

The quadrupole components
{\hat{\rm{Q}}_{i}^{\alpha\beta}}
in the function of the classical fluctuations can be found using
Eq. (51)
and
Eq. (121)
.
Using
Eq. (145)
, we can express
\Delta{\cal H}[{\bf{h}}_{i}]
in terms of the fluctuations that diagonalize the BBQ Hamiltonian. After performing a Fourier transform, and rewriting the Hamiltonian in the form of
Eq. (310)
, we get

\tilde{C}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}}]=\sqrt{N}\left(-\frac{4}{3}h_{\textbf{q}}^{yy}+\frac{2}{3}(h_{\textbf{q}}^{xx}+h_{\textbf{q}}^{zz})\right)\;,

(346)

where we neglected 2
nd
order terms in fluctuations, since they only contribute for
\textbf{q}=0
, and

\begin{matrix}\tilde{\textbf{N}}_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}}]^{T}=\begin{pmatrix}0,&\frac{i\sqrt{2}}{2}\xi^{1}_{\textbf{q}},&0,&-\frac{i\sqrt{2}}{2}\xi^{1}_{\textbf{q}}\end{pmatrix}\;,\\
\tilde{\textbf{N}}_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{-\textbf{q}}]=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\frac{i\sqrt{2}}{2}\xi^{1}_{-\textbf{q}}\\
0\\
-\frac{i\sqrt{2}}{2}\xi^{1}_{-\textbf{q}}\end{pmatrix}\;,\end{matrix}

(347)

where

\begin{matrix}\xi^{1}_{\textbf{q}}=(h^{xy}_{\textbf{q}}+h^{yx}_{\textbf{q}})&\;,&\xi^{2}_{\textbf{q}}=(h^{yz}_{\textbf{q}}+h^{zy}_{\textbf{q}})\;.\end{matrix}

(348)

The total quadrupole structure factor is given by
Eq. (344)
.
According to
Eq. (317)
and
Eq. (318)
, and using
Eq. (347)
, we obtain

\displaystyle S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{Q}}(\textbf{q}\neq 0)=

\displaystyle\frac{4}{\beta\omega_{\textbf{q},1}}+\frac{4}{\beta\omega_{\textbf{q},3}}+{\mathcal{O}}(T^{2})=\frac{8}{\beta\omega_{\textbf{q}}^{+}}+{\mathcal{O}}(T^{2})\;,

(349)

where we used
Eq. (140a)
.
and its spectral decomposition [
Eq. (316)
] becomes

\displaystyle S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{Q}}(\textbf{q}\neq 0,\omega)=\frac{8}{\beta\omega_{\textbf{q}}^{+}}\delta(\omega-\omega_{\textbf{q}}^{+})+{\mathcal{O}}(T^{2})\;.

(350)

D.5
Quadrupole moments: classical structure factor at
\textbf{q}=0

We now consider the quadrupole structure factor at the
\Gamma
–point, which is defined as

\displaystyle S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{Q}}(\textbf{q}=0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha\beta}{\langle{\hat{\rm{Q}}_{\textbf{q}=0}^{\alpha\beta}}{\hat{\rm{Q}}_{\textbf{q}=0}^{\beta\alpha}}\rangle}\;.

(351)

We follow the same procedure as depicted in
Appendix
D.1
. The relevant source term for quadrupole moments is given by
Eq. (345)
. We use
Eq. (127)
for the BBQ Hamiltonian as well as
Eq. (121)
and
Eq. (51)
to express
Eq. (345)
up to second order in terms of the fluctuations. For the total Hamiltonian given by
Eq. (153)
, and written in the form of
Eq. (320)
, we obtain

M_{\textbf{k}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]=\begin{pmatrix}A_{\textbf{k}}+\alpha_{1}&-B_{\textbf{k}}&0&\beta_{1}\\
-B_{\textbf{k}}&A_{\textbf{k}}+\alpha_{1}&\beta_{1}&0\\
0&\beta_{1}&A_{\textbf{k}}+\alpha_{2}&-B_{\textbf{k}}\\
\beta_{1}&0&-B_{\textbf{k}}&A_{\textbf{k}}+\alpha_{2}\\
\end{pmatrix}\;,

(352)

\begin{matrix}\textbf{N}_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]^{T}=\frac{-i}{2}\begin{pmatrix}-\xi^{1}_{\textbf{k}},&\xi^{1}_{\textbf{k}},&\xi^{2}_{\textbf{k}},&-\xi^{2}_{\textbf{k}}\end{pmatrix}\;,\\
\textbf{N}_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{-\textbf{k}}]=\frac{i}{2}\begin{pmatrix}-\xi^{1}_{-\textbf{k}}\\
\xi^{1}_{-\textbf{k}}\\
\xi^{2}_{-\textbf{k}}\\
-\xi^{2}_{-\textbf{k}}\end{pmatrix}\;,\end{matrix}

(353)

C[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]=\sqrt{N}\left(\frac{4}{3}h_{\textbf{q}=0,y}^{y}-\frac{2}{3}(h_{\textbf{q}=0,x}^{x}+h_{\textbf{q}=0,z}^{z})\right)\;,

(354)

where we define

\begin{matrix}\begin{matrix}\alpha_{1}=\frac{2}{\sqrt{N}}(h_{\textbf{q}=0,x}^{x}-h_{\textbf{q}=0,y}^{y})\;,&\alpha_{2}=\frac{2}{\sqrt{N}}(h_{\textbf{q}=0,z}^{z}-h_{\textbf{q}=0,y}^{y})\;,\end{matrix}\\
&\\
\begin{matrix}\beta_{1}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}(h_{\textbf{q}=0,z}^{x}+h_{\textbf{q}=0,x}^{z})\;,\end{matrix}\\
&\\
\begin{matrix}\xi^{1}_{\textbf{k}}=(h^{x}_{\textbf{k},y}+h^{y}_{\textbf{k},x})&\;,&\xi^{2}_{\textbf{k}}=(h^{y}_{\textbf{k},z}+h^{z}_{\textbf{k},y})\end{matrix}\;.\end{matrix}

(355)

We diagonalize
Eq. (352)
to obtain the eigenmodes. We find

\displaystyle\omega_{\textbf{k},1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]

\displaystyle=A_{\textbf{k}}+B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{+}+\Delta)\;,

(356a)

\displaystyle\omega_{\textbf{k},2}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]

\displaystyle=A_{\textbf{k}}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{+}+\Delta)\;,

(356b)

\displaystyle\omega_{\textbf{k},3}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]

\displaystyle=A_{\textbf{k}}+B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{+}-\Delta)\;,

(356c)

\displaystyle\omega_{\textbf{k},4}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]

\displaystyle=A_{\textbf{k}}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{+}-\Delta)\;,

(356d)

where

\begin{matrix}\alpha_{+}=\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}&\;,&\Delta=\sqrt{(\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2})^{2}+4\beta_{1}^{2}}\;.\end{matrix}

(357)

Finally, we use
Eq. (325)
and
Eq. (326)
to compute the quadrupole structure factor at the
\Gamma
–point given by
Eq. (351)
.
When calculating
Eq. (325)
and
Eq. (326)
, we use
Eq. (354)
and
Eq. (356)
, and for the last term, we simply invert
Eq. (352)
and multiply by the vectors expressed in
Eq. (353)
.
We obtain

\displaystyle S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{Q}}(\textbf{q}=0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{8}{\beta}\frac{1}{\omega^{+}_{0}}+\frac{8}{3}N-\frac{8}{\beta}\sum_{\textbf{k}}\left[\frac{1}{\omega^{+}_{\textbf{k}}}+\frac{1}{\omega^{-}_{\textbf{k}}}\right]+{\mathcal{O}}(T^{2})\;.

However, we note that at the
\Gamma
–point,
\omega^{+}_{0}=0
. Therefore, in order to get rid of confounding divergent terms, we rewrite the quadrupole structure factor as

\displaystyle S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{Q}}(\textbf{q}=0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{8}{\beta}\frac{1}{\omega^{-}_{0}}+\frac{8}{3}N-\frac{8}{\beta}\sum_{\textbf{k}\neq 0}\left[\frac{1}{\omega^{+}_{\textbf{k}}}+\frac{1}{\omega^{-}_{\textbf{k}}}\right]+{\mathcal{O}}(T^{2})\;.

Because the
\textbf{q}=0
contributions are coming from the ground state and happen for
\omega=0
, the spectral representation of
Section D.5
yields

\displaystyle S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{Q}}(\textbf{q}=0,\omega)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{8}{\beta}\frac{1}{\omega^{-}_{0}}\delta(\omega)+\frac{8}{3}N\delta(\omega)

\displaystyle-\frac{8}{\beta}\sum_{\textbf{k}\neq 0}\left[\frac{1}{\omega^{+}_{\textbf{k}}}+\frac{1}{\omega^{-}_{\textbf{k}}}\right]\delta(\omega)+{\mathcal{O}}(T^{2})\;.

Combining
Eq. (349)
and
Section D.5
, we obtain
Eq. (161)
. Considering their respective spectral representation given by
Eq. (350)
and
Section D.5
, we obtain
Eq. (163)
.

D.6
A-matrices: classical structure factor
\textbf{q}\neq 0

The matrix
{\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{\penalty\ \beta}^{\alpha}}
is the most fundamental object describing
the spins, and its structure factor
is defined by

\displaystyle S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{A}}(\textbf{q})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha\beta}{\langle{\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{\textbf{q}\penalty\ \beta}^{\alpha}}{\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{-\textbf{q}\penalty\ \alpha}^{\beta}}\rangle}\;.

(361)

We note that the sum on the contracted indices
\alpha,\beta
preserves
the full
U(3)
symmetry of the representation.
The corresponding source term is

\Delta{\cal H}[{\bf{h}}_{i}]=-\sum_{i}h_{i,\beta}^{\alpha}{\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{i\penalty\ \beta}^{\alpha}}\;.

(362)

The components of the A matrix
{\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{i\penalty\ \beta}^{\alpha}}
in the function of the classical fluctuations are given in
Eq. (121)
. After expressing them in the function of the fluctuations that diagonalize the BBQ Hamiltonian [
Eq. (145)
], performing a Fourier transform, and rewriting the total Hamiltonian [
Eq. (153)
] according to
Eq. (310)
, we get

\tilde{C}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}}]=\sqrt{N}h^{yy}_{\textbf{q}}\;,

(363)

where we neglected 2
nd
order terms in fluctuations, since they only contribute for
\textbf{q}=0
,

\begin{matrix}\tilde{\textbf{N}}_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}}]^{T}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{i\sqrt{2}}{2}\xi^{1}_{\textbf{q}},&\frac{i\sqrt{2}}{2}\xi^{1}_{\textbf{q}},&-\frac{i\sqrt{2}}{2}\xi^{2}_{\textbf{q}}&-\frac{i\sqrt{2}}{2}\xi^{2}_{\textbf{q}}\end{pmatrix}\;,\\
\tilde{\textbf{N}}_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{-\textbf{q}}]=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{i\sqrt{2}}{2}\xi^{1}_{-\textbf{q}}\\
\frac{i\sqrt{2}}{2}\xi^{1}_{-\textbf{q}}\\
-\frac{i\sqrt{2}}{2}\xi^{2}_{-\textbf{q}}\\
-\frac{i\sqrt{2}}{2}\xi^{2}_{-\textbf{q}}\end{pmatrix}\;,\end{matrix}

(364)

where

\begin{matrix}\xi^{1}_{\textbf{q}}=(h^{xy}_{\textbf{q}}+h^{yx}_{\textbf{q}})&\;,&\xi^{2}_{\textbf{q}}=(h^{yz}_{\textbf{q}}+h^{zy}_{\textbf{q}})\;.\end{matrix}

(365)

The total structure factor for A matrices is obtained by computing
Eq. (361)
.
According to
Eq. (318)
, and
Eq. (317)
, and using
Eq. (364)
, we obtain

\displaystyle S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{A}}(\textbf{q}\neq 0)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\beta\omega_{\textbf{q},1}}+\frac{1}{\beta\omega_{\textbf{q},2}}+\frac{1}{\beta\omega_{\textbf{q},3}}+\frac{1}{\beta\omega_{\textbf{q},4}}+{\mathcal{O}}(T^{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\beta\omega_{\textbf{q}}^{+}}+\frac{2}{\beta\omega_{\textbf{q}}^{-}}+{\mathcal{O}}(T^{2})\;,

(366)

where we used
Eq. (140)
.
It’s spectral decomposition is given by

\displaystyle S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{A}}(\textbf{q}\neq 0,\omega)=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\beta\omega_{\textbf{q}}^{+}}\delta(\omega-\omega_{\textbf{q}}^{+})+\frac{2}{\beta\omega_{\textbf{q}}^{-}}\delta(\omega-\omega_{\textbf{q}}^{-})

\displaystyle+{\mathcal{O}}(T^{2})\;.

(367)

Again replacing the eigenvalues by their expressions given in
Eq. (140)
, we have

\displaystyle S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{A}}(\textbf{q}\neq 0,\omega)=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\beta}\frac{1}{A_{\textbf{q}}+B_{\textbf{q}}}\delta(\omega-\omega_{\textbf{q}}^{+})

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{\beta}\frac{1}{A_{\textbf{q}}-B_{\textbf{q}}}\delta(\omega-\omega_{\textbf{q}}^{-})

\displaystyle+{\mathcal{O}}(T^{2})\;.

(368)

D.7
A-Matrices: classical structure factor at
\textbf{q}=0

We now consider the structure factor for the A–matrix at the
\Gamma
–point, which is defined as

\displaystyle S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{A}}(\textbf{q}=0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha\beta}{\langle{\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{\textbf{q}=0\penalty\ \beta}^{\alpha}}{\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{\textbf{q}=0\penalty\ \alpha}^{\beta}}\rangle}\;.

(369)

Again, we follow the procedure depicted in
Appendix

D.1
. The relevant source term for dipole moments is given by
Eq. (362)
. We use
Eq. (127)
for the BBQ Hamiltonian as well as
Eq. (121)
to express
Eq. (362)
up to second order in terms of the fluctuations. For the total Hamiltonian given in
Eq. (153)
, and written in the form of
Eq. (320)
, we obtain

M_{\textbf{k}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]=\begin{pmatrix}A_{\textbf{k}}-\alpha_{1}&-B_{\textbf{k}}&0&-\beta_{1}\\
-B_{\textbf{k}}&A_{\textbf{k}}-\alpha_{1}&-\beta_{2}&0\\
0&-\beta_{1}&A_{\textbf{k}}-\alpha_{2}&-B_{\textbf{k}}\\
-\beta_{2}&0&-B_{\textbf{k}}&A_{\textbf{k}}-\alpha_{2}\\
\end{pmatrix}\;,

(370)

\begin{matrix}\textbf{N}_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]^{T}=\frac{i}{2}\begin{pmatrix}-h^{x}_{\textbf{k},y},&h^{y}_{\textbf{k},x},&h^{y}_{\textbf{k},z},&-h^{z}_{\textbf{k},y}\end{pmatrix}\;,\\
\textbf{N}_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{-\textbf{k}}]=\frac{-i}{2}\begin{pmatrix}-h^{y}_{\textbf{k},x}\\
h^{x}_{\textbf{k},y}\\
h^{z}_{\textbf{k},y}\\
-h^{y}_{\textbf{k},z}\end{pmatrix}\;,\end{matrix}

(371)

C[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]=-\sqrt{N}h_{\textbf{q}=0,y}^{y}\;,

(372)

where we defined

\begin{matrix}\begin{matrix}\alpha_{1}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}(h_{\textbf{q}=0,x}^{x}-h_{\textbf{q}=0,y}^{y})\;,&\alpha_{2}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}(h_{\textbf{q}=0,z}^{z}-h_{\textbf{q}=0,y}^{y})\;,\end{matrix}\\
&\\
\begin{matrix}\beta_{1}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}h_{\textbf{q}=0,x}^{z}\;,&\beta_{2}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}h_{\textbf{q}=0,z}^{x}\;.\end{matrix}\end{matrix}

(373)

We diagonalize
Eq. (352)
to obtain the eigenmodes. We find

\displaystyle\omega_{\textbf{k},1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]

\displaystyle=A_{\textbf{k}}-\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{+}-\Delta^{-})\;,

(374a)

\displaystyle\omega_{\textbf{k},2}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]

\displaystyle=A_{\textbf{k}}-\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{+}+\Delta^{-})\;,

(374b)

\displaystyle\omega_{\textbf{k},3}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]

\displaystyle=A_{\textbf{k}}-\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{+}-\Delta^{+})\;,

(374c)

\displaystyle\omega_{\textbf{k},4}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]

\displaystyle=A_{\textbf{k}}-\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{+}+\Delta^{+})\;,

(374d)

where

\displaystyle\alpha_{+}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}\;,

\displaystyle\Delta^{-}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\alpha_{-}^{2}+4(B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}+\beta_{1}\beta_{2}-\sqrt{B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}(\alpha_{-}^{2}+\beta_{-}^{2})})}\;,

(375)

\displaystyle\Delta^{+}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\alpha_{-}^{2}+4(B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}+\beta_{1}\beta_{2}+\sqrt{B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}(\alpha_{-}^{2}+\beta_{-}^{2})})}\;,

with

\displaystyle\alpha_{-}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}\;,

\displaystyle\beta_{-}

\displaystyle=\beta_{1}-\beta_{2}\;.

(376)

Finally, we use
Eq. (326)
and
Eq. (325)
to compute the structure factor for the A–matrix at the
\Gamma
–point given by
Eq. (369)
. When calculating
Eq. (326)
and
Eq. (325)
, we use
Eq. (372)
and
Eq. (374)
, and for the last term, we simply invert
Eq. (370)
and multiply by the vectors in
Eq. (371)
.

\displaystyle S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{A}}(\textbf{q}=0)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\beta}\left[\frac{1}{\omega^{+}_{0}}+\frac{1}{\omega^{-}_{0}}\right]

\displaystyle+N-\frac{2}{\beta}\sum_{\textbf{k}}\left[\frac{1}{\omega^{+}_{\textbf{k}}}+\frac{1}{\omega^{-}_{\textbf{k}}}\right]+{\mathcal{O}}(T^{2})\;.

(377)

Again, just as for the quadrupole structure factor, we note that at the
\Gamma
–point,
\omega^{+}_{0}=0
. Therefore, in order to get rid of confounding divergent terms, we rewrite the structure factor as

\displaystyle S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{A}}(\textbf{q}=0)

\displaystyle=N-\frac{2}{\beta}\sum_{\textbf{k}\neq 0}\left[\frac{1}{\omega^{+}_{\textbf{k}}}+\frac{1}{\omega^{-}_{\textbf{k}}}\right]+{\mathcal{O}}(T^{2})\;.

(378)

Because the
\textbf{q}=0
contributions are coming from the ground state and happen for
\omega=0
, the spectral representation of
Eq. (378)
yields

\displaystyle S^{\sf CL}_{\rm{A}}(\textbf{q}=0,\omega)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N\delta(\omega)

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{\beta}\sum_{\textbf{k}\neq 0}\left[\frac{1}{\omega^{+}_{\textbf{k}}}+\frac{1}{\omega^{-}_{\textbf{k}}}\right]\delta(\omega)+{\mathcal{O}}(T^{2})\;.

Combining
Eq. (366)
and
Eq. (378)
, we obtain
. Considering their respective spectral representation given by
Eq. (367)
and
Section D.7
, we obtain
Eq. (166)
.

Appendix E
Bogolioubov
transformation

We here show how the Bogoliubov transformation that we present in
Section V
is performed.

A Bogolioubov transformation consists in finding new bosons
{\hat{\rm{v}}^{\dagger\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}}
and
{\hat{\rm{v}}_{\textbf{k}\alpha}}
expressed in terms of the bosons
{\hat{\rm{w}}^{\dagger\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}}
and
{\hat{\rm{w}}_{\textbf{k}\alpha}}
[
Eq. (175)
] , such that they diagonalize the Hamiltonian

{\cal H}_{\sf BBQ}\sim\sum_{\textbf{k}}\epsilon_{\textbf{k}}{\boldsymbol{\hat{\rm{v}}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}}{\boldsymbol{\hat{\rm{v}}}_{\textbf{k}}}\;.

(380)

Let us assume that the components are given by

\displaystyle{\hat{\rm{v}}_{\textbf{k}\alpha}}=

\displaystyle{U_{\textbf{k}}}_{\alpha}^{\penalty\ \beta}{\hat{\rm{w}}_{\textbf{k}\beta}}\;,

\displaystyle{\hat{\rm{v}}^{\dagger\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}}=

\displaystyle{\hat{\rm{w}}^{\dagger\beta}_{\textbf{k}}}{U^{\dagger\penalty\ \alpha}_{\textbf{k}\beta}}\;,

(381)

where
{U_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }
is the transformation from basis made out of bosons expressed by time-reversal basis states to the basis in which the Hamiltonian is diagonal.
Requiring them to have bosonic commutation relations [
Eq. (176)
], leads to

\displaystyle{\left[{\hat{\rm{v}}_{\textbf{k}\alpha}},{\hat{\rm{v}}^{\dagger\beta}_{\textbf{q}}}\right]}

\displaystyle={\left[{U_{\textbf{k}}}_{\alpha}^{\penalty\ \gamma}{\hat{\rm{w}}_{\textbf{k}\gamma}},{U^{\dagger\penalty\ \beta}_{\textbf{q}\eta}}{\hat{\rm{w}}^{\dagger\eta}_{\textbf{q}}}\right]}

\displaystyle={U_{\textbf{k}}}_{\alpha}^{\penalty\ \gamma}{U^{\dagger\penalty\ \beta}_{\textbf{q}\eta}}{\left[{\hat{\rm{w}}_{\textbf{k}\gamma}},{\hat{\rm{w}}^{\dagger\eta}_{\textbf{q}}}\right]}

\displaystyle={U_{\textbf{k}}}_{\alpha}^{\penalty\ \gamma}{U^{\dagger\penalty\ \beta}_{\textbf{q}\eta}}{\gamma_{0}}_{\gamma}^{\penalty\ \eta}\delta_{\textbf{k}\textbf{q}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}{\gamma_{0}}_{\alpha}^{\penalty\ \beta}\delta_{\textbf{k}\textbf{q}}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\

\displaystyle{U_{\textbf{k}}}_{\alpha}^{\penalty\ \gamma}{\gamma_{0}}_{\gamma}^{\penalty\ \eta}{U^{\dagger\penalty\ \beta}_{\textbf{k}\eta}}={\gamma_{0}}_{\alpha}^{\penalty\ \beta}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\

\displaystyle{\gamma_{0}}_{\gamma}^{\penalty\ \eta}{U^{\dagger\penalty\ \beta}_{\textbf{k}\eta}}{\gamma_{0}}_{\beta}^{\alpha}={U^{-1}_{\textbf{k}}}_{\gamma}^{\penalty\ \alpha}\;,

(382)

where
{\gamma_{0}}
is defined in
Eq. (177)
, and where we used the fact that

{\gamma_{0}}={\gamma_{0}}^{-1}\;.

(383)

In the compact form,
Eq. (382)
becomes

{\gamma_{0}}{U^{\dagger\penalty\ }_{\textbf{k}}}{\gamma_{0}}={U^{-1}_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }\;.

(384)

We see that the transformation
{U_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }
is not unitary,
{U^{-1}_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }\neq{U^{\dagger\penalty\ }_{\textbf{k}}}
, and that we shall use
Eq. (384)
to find the inverse transformation.

Inverting
Eq. (381)
and plugging it into the Hamiltonian leads us to look for a transformation
{U_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }
such that
{U_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }{\gamma_{0}}M_{\textbf{k}}{U^{-1}_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }
is diagonal. If we define
D_{\textbf{k}}
as being a diagonal matrix, we can write

\displaystyle{U_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }{\gamma_{0}}{M_{\textbf{k}}}{U^{-1}_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }

\displaystyle=D_{\textbf{k}}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ {\gamma_{0}}_{\nu}^{\penalty\ \alpha}{M_{\textbf{k}}}_{\alpha}^{\penalty\ \beta}{U^{-1}_{\textbf{k}}}_{\beta}^{\penalty\ i}

\displaystyle={U^{-1}_{\textbf{k}}}_{\nu}^{\penalty\ i}{D_{\textbf{k}}}_{i}^{i}\penalty\ \penalty\ \rm{for}\penalty\ i=1,2,3,4\;,

(385)

where we see that
{U^{-1}_{\textbf{k}}}_{\nu}^{\penalty\ i}
is an eigenvector of
{\gamma_{0}}{M_{\textbf{k}}}
with eigenvalue
{D_{\textbf{k}}}_{i}^{i}
.
Eq. (385)
is rewritten as
Eq. (178)
in the main text. This means that we need to diagonalize
{\gamma_{0}}M_{\textbf{k}}
and that the corresponding eigenvectors are the column of the matrix
{U^{-1}_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }
.

Finding the Bogolioubov transformation reduces then to find the eigenvalues and eigenvectors of the system in
Eq. (178)
.
Since
Eq. (178)
consists of twice the same system, we only need to solve it once, and we only consider

\sigma_{z}m_{\textbf{k}}e_{i}=\epsilon_{\textbf{k},i}e_{i}\penalty\ \penalty\ i=1,2\;,

(386)

where

\begin{matrix}\sigma_{z}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\\
\end{pmatrix}&,\penalty\ &m_{\textbf{k}}=\begin{pmatrix}A_{\textbf{k}}&-B_{\textbf{k}}\\
B_{\textbf{k}}&-A_{\textbf{k}}\end{pmatrix}\end{matrix}\;,

(387)

and where
\sigma_{z}
plays the role of
\gamma_{0}
but for the two independent subsystems for
\begin{pmatrix}{\hat{\rm{a}}_{-\textbf{k}}},&{\hat{\rm{a}}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}\end{pmatrix}
and
\begin{pmatrix}{\hat{\rm{b}}_{-\textbf{k}}},&{\hat{\rm{b}}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}\end{pmatrix}
. The eigenvalues
\epsilon_{\textbf{k},1/2}
of
\sigma_{z}m_{\textbf{k}}
are given in
Eq. (180)
.
The eigenvectors are given by

\begin{matrix}e_{1}&=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{1}\\
1\end{pmatrix}&,\penalty\ &e_{2}&=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{2}\\
1\end{pmatrix}\end{matrix}\;,

(388)

in the basis
\{{\hat{\rm{a}}_{\textbf{k}}},{\hat{\rm{a}}_{-\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}\}
and where we define

\alpha_{i}=-\frac{A_{\textbf{k}}+\epsilon_{\textbf{k},i}}{B_{\textbf{k}}}\;.

(389)

The columns of the matrix
{U^{-1}_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }
are given by the eigenvectors

{U^{-1}_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{1}&\alpha_{2}\\
1&1\end{pmatrix}\;.

(390)

Using
Eq. (384)
, we can calculate
{U_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }
as follows:

\displaystyle{U_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }

\displaystyle=\sigma_{z}{U^{\dagger-1}_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }\sigma_{z}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{1}&-1\\
-\alpha_{2}&1\end{pmatrix}\;.

(391)

Using
Eq. (381)
, the new bosons that diagonalize the Hamiltonian are given by

\displaystyle{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}}={\hat{\rm{v}}_{\textbf{k}1}}

\displaystyle={U_{\textbf{k}}}_{1}^{\penalty\ 1}{\hat{\rm{w}}_{\textbf{k}1}}+{U_{\textbf{k}}}_{1}^{\penalty\ 2}{\hat{\rm{w}}_{\textbf{k}2}}=\alpha_{1}{\hat{\rm{a}}_{\textbf{k}}}-{\hat{\rm{a}}_{-\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}\;,

(392a)

\displaystyle{\hat{\alpha}_{-\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}={\hat{\rm{v}}_{\textbf{k}2}}

\displaystyle={U_{\textbf{k}}}_{2}^{\penalty\ 1}{\hat{\rm{w}}_{\textbf{k}1}}+{U_{\textbf{k}}}_{2}^{\penalty\ 2}{\hat{\rm{w}}_{\textbf{k}2}}=-\alpha_{2}{\hat{\rm{a}}_{\textbf{k}}}+{\hat{\rm{a}}_{-\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}\;,

(392b)

\displaystyle{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}={\hat{\rm{v}}^{\dagger 1}_{\textbf{k}}}

\displaystyle={\hat{\rm{w}}^{\dagger 1}_{\textbf{k}}}{U^{\dagger\penalty\ 1}_{\textbf{k}1}}+{\hat{\rm{w}}^{\dagger 2}_{\textbf{k}}}{U^{\dagger\penalty\ 1}_{\textbf{k}2}}=\alpha_{1}{\hat{\rm{a}}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}-{\hat{\rm{a}}_{-\textbf{k}}}\;,

(392c)

\displaystyle{\hat{\alpha}_{-\textbf{k}}}={\hat{\rm{v}}^{\dagger 2}_{\textbf{k}}}

\displaystyle={\hat{\rm{w}}^{\dagger 1}_{\textbf{k}}}{U^{\dagger\penalty\ 2}_{\textbf{k}1}}+{\hat{\rm{w}}^{\dagger 2}_{\textbf{k}}}{U^{\dagger\penalty\ 2}_{\textbf{k}2}}=-\alpha_{2}{\hat{\rm{a}}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}+{\hat{\rm{a}}_{-\textbf{k}}}\;.

(392d)

For instance, we note that we should have
{\hat{\rm{v}}_{\textbf{k}1}}={\hat{\rm{v}}^{\dagger 2}_{-\textbf{k}}}
i.e
{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}}(\textbf{k})={\hat{\alpha}_{-\textbf{k}}}(-\textbf{k})
. However, we see that it is not the case

{\hat{\rm{v}}_{\textbf{k}1}}=\alpha_{1}{\hat{\rm{a}}_{\textbf{k}}}-{\hat{\rm{a}}_{-\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}\neq-\alpha_{2}{\hat{\rm{a}}_{-\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}+{\hat{\rm{a}}_{\textbf{k}}}={\hat{\rm{v}}^{\dagger 2}_{-\textbf{k}}}\;.

(393)

For it to be the case, we see that we need the matrix element of the transformation to be

\displaystyle{U_{\textbf{k}}}_{1}^{\penalty\ 1}={U^{\dagger\penalty\ 2}_{-\textbf{k}2}}\;,

(394a)

\displaystyle{U_{\textbf{k}}}_{1}^{\penalty\ 2}={U^{\dagger\penalty\ 2}_{-\textbf{k}1}}\;.

(394b)

To solve this issue, we can assume that we can multiply the eigenvectors by some parameters,
{a}
and
{b}
for instance, such that
Eq. (394)
is satisfied

\begin{matrix}e_{1}&=a\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{1}\\
1\end{pmatrix}&,\penalty\ &e_{2}&=b\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{2}\\
1\end{pmatrix}\end{matrix}\;.

(395)

And
{U^{-1}_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }
is given by

{U^{-1}_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }=\begin{pmatrix}a\alpha_{1}&b\alpha_{2}\\
a&b\end{pmatrix}\;.

(396)

Using
Eq. (384)
, we can calculate
{U_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }
as follows:

\displaystyle{U_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }

\displaystyle=\sigma_{z}{U^{\dagger-1}_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }\sigma_{z}=\begin{pmatrix}a\alpha_{1}&-a\\
-b\alpha_{2}&b\end{pmatrix}\;.

(397)

We also have

\displaystyle{U^{\dagger\penalty\ }_{\textbf{k}}}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}a\alpha_{1}&-b\alpha_{2}\\
-a&b\end{pmatrix}\;.

(398)

Note that the coefficients
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}
depend on
k
through
\epsilon_{\textbf{k}}
. However we have
\epsilon_{\textbf{k}}=\epsilon_{-\textbf{k}}
and the dependency in
k
for
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}
has been dropped.

Eq. (394)
implies then

\begin{matrix}{U_{\textbf{k}}}_{1}^{\penalty\ 1}&=&{U^{\dagger\penalty\ 2}_{-\textbf{k}2}}\;,\\
a\alpha_{1}&=&b\;,\\
a\frac{-A_{\textbf{k}}-\sqrt{A_{\textbf{k}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}}}{B_{\textbf{k}}}&=&b\;,\\
\frac{a}{-B_{\textbf{k}}}&=&\frac{b}{A_{\textbf{k}}+\sqrt{A_{\textbf{k}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}}}\;,\end{matrix}

(399a)

and

\begin{matrix}{U_{\textbf{k}}}_{1}^{\penalty\ 2}&=&{U^{\dagger\penalty\ 2}_{-\textbf{k}1}}\;,\\
-b\alpha_{2}&=&-a\;,\\
-b\frac{-A_{\textbf{k}}+\sqrt{A_{\textbf{k}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}}}{B_{\textbf{k}}}&=&-a\;,\\
\frac{b}{-B_{\textbf{k}}}&=&\frac{-a}{-A_{\textbf{k}}+\sqrt{A_{\textbf{k}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}}}\;.\end{matrix}

(399b)

We see that if we multiply the last line of
Eq. (399b)
by
\frac{-B_{\textbf{k}}}{A_{\textbf{k}}+\sqrt{A_{\textbf{k}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}}}
we get

\displaystyle\frac{-B_{\textbf{k}}}{A_{\textbf{k}}+\sqrt{A_{\textbf{k}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}}}

\displaystyle\frac{b}{-B_{\textbf{k}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-B_{\textbf{k}}}{A_{\textbf{k}}+\sqrt{A_{\textbf{k}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}}}\frac{-a}{-A_{\textbf{k}}+\sqrt{A_{\textbf{k}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}}}\;,

(400a)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\frac{b}{A_{\textbf{k}}+\sqrt{A_{\textbf{k}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}}}=\frac{a}{-B_{\textbf{k}}}\;.

(400b)

We note that
Eq. (400b)
is exactly the same condition as in the last line of
Eq. (399a)
. This makes sense, because the 1
st
condition, namely
{U_{\textbf{k}}}_{1}^{\penalty\ 1}={U^{\dagger\penalty\ 2}_{-\textbf{k}2}}
is correlated the the second one
{U_{\textbf{k}}}_{1}^{\penalty\ 2}={U^{\dagger\penalty\ 2}_{-\textbf{k}1}}
, as the components
{U_{\textbf{k}}}_{1}^{\penalty\ 1},\penalty\ {U_{\textbf{k}}}_{1}^{\penalty\ 2}
are not independent, as they need to be eigenvectors, and nor are the components
{U^{\dagger\penalty\ 2}_{-\textbf{k}2}},\penalty\ {U^{\dagger\penalty\ 2}_{-\textbf{k}1}}
. We could also choose the 2
nd
solution, as up to scalar multiplication, it gives the same eigenvectors. By normalizing the eigenvectors, we then get rid of this.
This means, for instance, that we can choose

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle=-B_{\textbf{k}}\;,

(401)

\displaystyle b

\displaystyle=A_{\textbf{k}}+\sqrt{A_{\textbf{k}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}}\;.

(402)

In this case, the eigenvectors become

\begin{matrix}e_{1}&=\begin{pmatrix}\Delta_{\textbf{k}}\\
-B_{\textbf{k}}\end{pmatrix}&,\penalty\ &e_{2}&=\begin{pmatrix}-B_{\textbf{k}}\\
\Delta_{\textbf{k}}\end{pmatrix}\end{matrix}\;,

(403)

where
\Delta_{\textbf{k}}
is given
Eq. (183)
.
And the transformation matrix becomes

{U^{-1}_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }=\begin{pmatrix}\Delta_{\textbf{k}}&-B_{\textbf{k}}\\
-B_{\textbf{k}}&\Delta_{\textbf{k}}\end{pmatrix}\;.

(404)

Using
Eq. (384)
, we can calculate
{U_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }
as follows:

\displaystyle{U_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }

\displaystyle=\sigma_{z}{U^{\dagger-1}_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }\sigma_{z}=\begin{pmatrix}\Delta_{\textbf{k}}&B_{\textbf{k}}\\
B_{\textbf{k}}&\Delta_{\textbf{k}}\end{pmatrix}\;.

(405)

We also have

\displaystyle{U^{\dagger\penalty\ }_{\textbf{k}}}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\Delta_{\textbf{k}}&B_{\textbf{k}}\\
B_{\textbf{k}}&\Delta_{\textbf{k}}\end{pmatrix}\;.

(406)

Using
Eq. (381)
, the new bosons that diagonalize the Hamiltonian are given by

\displaystyle{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}}={\hat{\rm{v}}_{\textbf{k}1}}=

\displaystyle{U_{\textbf{k}}}_{1}^{\penalty\ 1}{\hat{\rm{w}}_{\textbf{k}1}}+{U_{\textbf{k}}}_{1}^{\penalty\ 2}{\hat{\rm{w}}_{\textbf{k}2}}=\Delta_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\rm{a}}_{\textbf{k}}}+B_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\rm{a}}_{-\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}\;,

(407a)

\displaystyle{\hat{\alpha}_{-\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}={\hat{\rm{v}}_{\textbf{k}2}}=

\displaystyle{U_{\textbf{k}}}_{2}^{\penalty\ 1}{\hat{\rm{w}}_{\textbf{k}1}}+{U_{\textbf{k}}}_{2}^{\penalty\ 2}{\hat{\rm{w}}_{\textbf{k}2}}=B_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\rm{a}}_{\textbf{k}}}+\Delta_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\rm{a}}_{-\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}\;,

(407b)

\displaystyle{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}={\hat{\rm{v}}^{\dagger 1}_{\textbf{k}}}=

\displaystyle{\hat{\rm{w}}^{\dagger 1}_{\textbf{k}}}{U^{\dagger\penalty\ 1}_{\textbf{k}1}}+{\hat{\rm{w}}^{\dagger 2}_{\textbf{k}}}{U^{\dagger\penalty\ 1}_{\textbf{k}2}}=\Delta_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\rm{a}}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}+B_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\rm{a}}_{-\textbf{k}}}\;,

(407c)

\displaystyle{\hat{\alpha}_{-\textbf{k}}}={\hat{\rm{v}}^{\dagger 2}_{\textbf{k}}}=

\displaystyle{\hat{\rm{w}}^{\dagger 1}_{\textbf{k}}}{U^{\dagger\penalty\ 2}_{\textbf{k}1}}+{\hat{\rm{w}}^{\dagger 2}_{\textbf{k}}}{U^{\dagger\penalty\ 2}_{\textbf{k}2}}=B_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\rm{a}}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}+\Delta_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\rm{a}}_{-\textbf{k}}}\;.

(407d)

We see that now we indeed have
{\hat{\rm{v}}_{\textbf{k}1}}={\hat{\rm{v}}^{\dagger 2}_{-\textbf{k}}}
, i.e.,
{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}}(\textbf{k})={\hat{\alpha}_{-\textbf{k}}}(-\textbf{k})={\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}}
.
However, we still need to normalize the new bosons. Indeed they should also satisfy bosonic commutation relations

\displaystyle{\left[{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}},{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}\right]}

\displaystyle={\left[\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}(\Delta_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\rm{a}}_{\textbf{k}}}+B_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\rm{a}}_{-\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}),\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}(\Delta_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\rm{a}}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}+B_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\rm{a}}_{-\textbf{k}}})\right]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}(\Delta_{\textbf{k}}^{2}{\left[{\hat{\rm{a}}_{\textbf{k}}},{\hat{\rm{a}}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}\right]}+B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}{\left[{\hat{\rm{a}}_{-\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}},{\hat{\rm{a}}_{-\textbf{k}}}\right]})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}(\Delta_{\textbf{k}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2})\overset{!}{=}1

(408a)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ N

\displaystyle=\Delta_{\textbf{k}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}\;.

(408b)

Finally, the transformation matrix becomes

{U_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{\textbf{k}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}}}\begin{pmatrix}\Delta_{\textbf{k}}&B_{\textbf{k}}\\
B_{\textbf{k}}&\Delta_{\textbf{k}}\end{pmatrix}\;.

(409)

The inverse [
Eq. (384)
] holds

{U^{-1}_{\textbf{k}}}^{\penalty\ }=\sigma_{z}{U^{\dagger\penalty\ }_{\textbf{k}}}\sigma_{z}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{\textbf{k}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}}}\begin{pmatrix}\Delta_{\textbf{k}}&-B_{\textbf{k}}\\
-B_{\textbf{k}}&\Delta_{\textbf{k}}\end{pmatrix}\;.

(410)

By inverting the Bogolibov transformation [
Eq. (381)
], we can express the old bosons in terms of the new Bogolibov bosons using
Eq. (410)
,

\displaystyle{\hat{\rm{a}}_{\textbf{k}}}{}={\hat{\rm{w}}_{\textbf{k}1}}=

\displaystyle{U^{-1}_{\textbf{k}}}_{1}^{\penalty\ \beta}{\hat{\rm{v}}_{\textbf{k}\beta}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{\textbf{k}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}}}(\Delta_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}}-B_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\alpha}_{-\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}})\>,

(411a)

\displaystyle{\hat{\rm{a}}_{-\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{}={\hat{\rm{w}}_{\textbf{k}2}}=

\displaystyle{U^{-1}_{\textbf{k}}}_{2}^{\penalty\ \beta}{\hat{\rm{v}}_{\textbf{k}\beta}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{\textbf{k}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}}}(-B_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}}+\Delta_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\alpha}_{-\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}})\;,

(411b)

\displaystyle{\hat{\rm{a}}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{}={\hat{\rm{w}}^{\dagger 1}_{\textbf{k}}}=

\displaystyle{\hat{\rm{v}}^{\dagger\beta}_{\textbf{k}}}{U^{\dagger-1}_{\textbf{k}}}_{\beta}^{\penalty\ 1}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{\textbf{k}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}}}(\Delta_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}-B_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\alpha}_{-\textbf{k}}})\;,

(411c)

\displaystyle{\hat{\rm{a}}_{-\textbf{k}}}{}={\hat{\rm{w}}^{\dagger 2}_{\textbf{k}}}=

\displaystyle{\hat{\rm{v}}^{\dagger\beta}_{\textbf{k}}}{U^{\dagger-1}_{\textbf{k}}}_{\beta}^{\penalty\ 1}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{\textbf{k}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}}}(-B_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}+\Delta_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\alpha}_{-\textbf{k}}})\;.

(411d)

For the other part of the Hamiltonian containing the
{\hat{\rm{b}}^{\dagger}}
bosons, the problem is exactly the same, and therefore, we can just use the solutions we found above.
The eigenvalues
\epsilon_{\textbf{k},3}
and
\epsilon_{\textbf{k},4}
associated to the Bogolibov bosons for the
\begin{pmatrix}{\hat{\rm{b}}_{-\textbf{k}}},&{\hat{\rm{b}}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}\end{pmatrix}
subsystem are given by
Eq. (180)
.
To express the old bosons in terms of the new Bogolibov bosons, we can just use
Eq. (411)
:

\displaystyle{\hat{\rm{b}}_{\textbf{k}}}{}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{\textbf{k}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}}}(\Delta_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}}}-B_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\beta}_{-\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}})\;,

(412a)

\displaystyle{\hat{\rm{b}}_{-\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{\textbf{k}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}}}(-B_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}}}+\Delta_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\beta}_{-\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}})\;,

(412b)

\displaystyle{\hat{\rm{b}}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{\textbf{k}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}}}(\Delta_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}-B_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\beta}_{-\textbf{k}}})\;,

(412c)

\displaystyle{\hat{\rm{b}}_{-\textbf{k}}}{}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{\textbf{k}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}}}(-B_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}+\Delta_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\beta}_{-\textbf{k}}})\;.

(412d)

There is a constant term coming from the Bogoliubov transformation [
Eq. (411)
and
Eq. (412)
].

For the bosons
{\hat{\rm{a}}_{\pm\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{},{\hat{\rm{a}}_{\pm\textbf{k}}}{}
it holds:

\begin{matrix}-A_{\textbf{k}}+\sqrt{A_{\textbf{k}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}}=-A_{\textbf{k}}+\epsilon_{\textbf{k},1}\;,\end{matrix}

(413a)

and for the bosons
{\hat{\rm{b}}_{\pm\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{},{\hat{\rm{b}}_{\pm\textbf{k}}}{}
:

\begin{matrix}-A_{\textbf{k}}+\sqrt{A_{\textbf{k}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{k}}^{2}}=-A_{\textbf{k}}+\epsilon_{\textbf{k},3}\;,\end{matrix}

(413b)

where we use
Eq. (180)
.

After performing the Bogolibov transformation, the Hamiltonian becomes

\displaystyle{\cal H}=

\displaystyle E_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\left[\sum_{\textbf{k}}\epsilon_{\textbf{k},1}{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}}+\epsilon_{\textbf{k},1}{\hat{\alpha}_{-\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{\hat{\alpha}_{-\textbf{k}}}-A_{\textbf{k}}+\epsilon_{\textbf{k},1}\right]

\displaystyle+\left.\epsilon_{\textbf{k},3}{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}}}+\epsilon_{\textbf{k},3}{\hat{\beta}_{-\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{\hat{\beta}_{-\textbf{k}}}-A_{\textbf{k}}+\epsilon_{\textbf{k},3}\right]\;,

(414)

that we can rewrite as

\displaystyle{\cal H}=

\displaystyle E_{0}+\left[\sum_{\textbf{k}}\epsilon_{\textbf{k},1}({\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}}+\frac{1}{2})+\epsilon_{\textbf{k},3}({\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}}}+\frac{1}{2})-A_{\textbf{k}}\right]\;,

(415)

where
E_{0}
is given in
Eq. (125)
.
Eq. (415)
is given in the main text by
Eq. (184)
, where we used the fact that
\epsilon_{\textbf{k},1}=\epsilon_{\textbf{k},3}
[
Eq. (180)
], since they are the eigenvalues of an identical problem.

Appendix F
Dynamical structure factors within zero–temperature quantum theory

In this Appendix, we present the outline of the method used to calculate the zero–temperature quantum structure factors in
Section V.2
.

In
Appendix
F.1
, we first present how to calculate dynamical structure factors at finite energy through the explicit calculation of matrix elements within a multiple–Boson expansion, and its application to dipole [
Appendix
F.2
], quadrupole [
Appendix
F.3
], and A–matrix moments [
Appendix
F.4
].

In
Appendix
F.5
, we explain how the calculation for the static structure factors (
\omega=0
) can also be computed through functional derivatives of the ground–state energy, in order to account for the ground–state and zero–point energy contribution at
\textbf{q}=0
. We show calculations for the dipole [
Appendix
F.6
], quadrupole [
Appendix
F.7
], and A–matrix moments [
Appendix
F.8
].

F.1
Quantum structure factors at general values of
q

The definition of the structure factor is given by
Eq. (187)
and its components by

S^{\sf QM}_{\rm{O}}(\textbf{q},\omega)^{\alpha\mu}_{\beta\nu}=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}t}{2\pi}e^{i\omega t}{\langle\hat{O}_{\textbf{q},\beta}^{\alpha}(t)\hat{O}_{-\textbf{q},\nu}^{\mu}(0)\rangle}\;.

(416)

where in our case, the averages
{\langle\hat{O}_{\textbf{q},\beta}^{\alpha}(t)\hat{O}_{-\textbf{q},\nu}^{\mu}(0)\rangle}
are taken on the ground state.
We can rewrite the time dependency of
\hat{O}_{\textbf{q},\beta}^{\alpha}(t)
in the Heisenberg picture using the time evolution operator, and we obtain

\hat{O}_{\textbf{q},\beta}^{\alpha}(t)=e^{\frac{i\hat{{\cal H}}t}{\hbar}}\hat{O}_{\textbf{q},\beta}^{\alpha}(0)e^{\frac{-i\hat{{\cal H}}t}{\hbar}}\;.

(417)

For a complete basis
\{{\left|\nu\right\rangle}\}
of Hilbert space, the closure relation holds

\sum_{\nu}{\left|\nu\right\rangle}{\left\langle\nu\right|}=1\;.

(418)

By using
Eq. (417)
and inserting the closure relation,
Eq. (418)
twice in
Eq. (416)
, we get

\displaystyle S^{\alpha\mu}_{\rm{O},\beta\mu}(\textbf{q},\omega)

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}t}{2\pi}e^{i\omega t}\langle e^{\frac{i\hat{{\cal H}}t}{\hbar}}\sum_{\nu}{\left|\nu\right\rangle}{\left\langle\nu\right|}\hat{O}_{\textbf{q},\beta}^{\alpha}(0)\sum_{\mu}{\left|\mu\right\rangle}

\displaystyle\times{\left\langle\mu\right|}e^{\frac{-i\hat{{\cal H}}t}{\hbar}}\hat{O}_{-\textbf{q},\nu}^{\mu}(0)\rangle+S^{\sf GS}_{\rm{O}}(\textbf{q}=0,\omega)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu}{\left\langle 0\right|}\hat{O}_{\textbf{q},\beta}^{\alpha}(0){\left|\mu\right\rangle}{\left\langle\mu\right|}\hat{O}_{-\textbf{q},\nu}^{\mu}(0){\left|0\right\rangle}\delta(\omega-\epsilon_{\mu})

\displaystyle+S^{\sf GS}_{\rm{O}}(\textbf{q}=0,\omega)\;,

(419)

where we assumed that
{\left|\nu\right\rangle}
is an eigenstate of the Hamiltonian of energy
E_{\nu}=\hbar\epsilon_{\nu}
and used

\displaystyle e^{\frac{i\hat{{\cal H}}t}{\hbar}}{\left|\nu\right\rangle}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(i\hat{{\cal H}}t)^{n}}{n\!}{\left|\nu\right\rangle}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(iE_{\nu}t)^{n}}{n\!}{\left|\nu\right\rangle}

\displaystyle=e^{\frac{iE_{\nu}t}{\hbar}}{\left|\nu\right\rangle}=e^{i\epsilon_{\nu}t}{\left|\nu\right\rangle}\;,

(420)

and where
S^{\sf GS}_{\rm{O}}(\textbf{q}=0,\omega)
represents the ground state and zero-point energy contribution to the structure factor, as explained below.

In order to compute
Eq. (419)
, we first note that, in our case, the excited states, for all values of
k

\displaystyle{\left|\alpha_{\textbf{k}}\right\rangle}={\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{\left|0_{\alpha}\right\rangle}\;,

(421)

\displaystyle{\left|\beta_{\textbf{k}}\right\rangle}={\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{\left|0_{\beta}\right\rangle}\;,

(422)

form a complete basis, where
{\left|0_{\alpha}\right\rangle}
is the Bogoliubov ground state for the
{\hat{\alpha}}
bosons, i.e.,
{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}}{\left|0_{\alpha}\right\rangle}=0
, and similarly for the
{\hat{\beta}}
bosons. Since the Hilbert space consists of the direct product
{\left|{\alpha}\right\rangle}\oplus{\left|{\beta}\right\rangle}
,we can replace

\sum_{\mu}{\left|\mu\right\rangle}{\left\langle\mu\right|}\rightarrow\sum_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{\left|0_{\alpha}\right\rangle}{\left\langle 0_{\alpha}\right|}{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}}+\sum_{\textbf{k}}{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{\left|0_{\beta}\right\rangle}{\left\langle 0_{\beta}\right|}{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}}}=1\;.

(423)

in
Eq. (419)
.
However, by replacing
Eq. (418)
by
Eq. (423)
in
Eq. (419)
, we account for the 1
st
excited states and we therefore disregard the ground state and zero-point energy contribution to the structure factor, which is expressed by the term
S^{\sf GS}_{\rm{O}}(\textbf{q}=0,\omega)
in
Eq. (419)
. The ground state and zero-point energy only contribute at
\textbf{q}=0
and
\omega=0
. We present how to calculate it below in
Appendix
F.5
.

F.2
Dipole moments: quantum structure factor at general values of
q

We consider first the dynamical spin structure factor

S^{\sf QM}_{\rm{S}}(\textbf{q},\omega)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}t}{2\pi}e^{i\omega t}\sum_{\mu}{\langle{\hat{\rm{S}}_{\textbf{q}}^{\mu}}(t){\hat{\rm{S}}_{-\textbf{q}}^{\mu}}(0)\rangle}\;.

(424)

Substituting
Eq. (173)
in the expression for spin
operators,
Eq. (50)
, and keeping terms to linear order, we find

\displaystyle{\hat{\rm{S}}_{i}^{x}}

\displaystyle\simeq i({\hat{\rm{b}}_{i}^{\dagger}}-{\hat{\rm{b}}_{i}})\;,

(425a)

\displaystyle{\hat{\rm{S}}_{i}^{y}}

\displaystyle\simeq 0\;,

(425b)

\displaystyle{\hat{\rm{S}}_{i}^{z}}

\displaystyle\simeq-i({\hat{\rm{a}}_{i}^{\dagger}}-{\hat{\rm{a}}_{i}})\;.

(425c)

Performing a Fourier transform and using the Bogoliubov transformation
Eq. (182)
,
we can express these as

\displaystyle{\hat{\rm{S}}_{\textbf{q}}^{x}}

\displaystyle\simeq i\xi_{\sf{S}}(\textbf{q})({\hat{\beta}_{-\textbf{q}}^{\dagger}}-{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{q}}})\;,

(426a)

\displaystyle{\hat{\rm{S}}_{\textbf{q}}^{y}}

\displaystyle\simeq 0\;,

(426b)

\displaystyle{\hat{\rm{S}}_{\textbf{q}}^{z}}

\displaystyle\simeq-i\xi_{\sf{S}}(\textbf{q})({\hat{\alpha}_{-\textbf{q}}^{\dagger}}-{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}}})\;,

(426c)

where
\xi(\textbf{q})
is the coherence factor

\xi_{\sf{S}}(\textbf{q})=\frac{\Delta_{\textbf{q}}+B_{\textbf{q}}}{\sqrt{\Delta_{\textbf{q}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{q}}^{2}}}\;.

(427)

Using
Eq. (419)
, we can then calculate the structure factor for dipole moments as

\displaystyle S^{\sf QM}_{\rm{S}}(\textbf{q},\omega)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu,{\textbf{k}}}{\left|{\left\langle n_{\textbf{k}}\right|}{\hat{\rm{S}}_{\textbf{q}}^{\mu}}{\left|0\right\rangle}\right|}^{2}\delta(\omega-\omega_{n_{\textbf{k}}})+S^{\sf GS}_{\rm{S}}(\textbf{q}=0,\omega)\;,

(428)

where
{\left|0\right\rangle}
is the FQ ground state [
Eq. (114)
],
and

{\left|n_{\textbf{k}}\right\rangle}={\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{\left|0\right\rangle}\otimes{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{\left|0\right\rangle}\;.

(429)

represents the first excited states where
\otimes
implies a direct product, as the bosons
{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}}^{\dagger}}
and
{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{q}}^{\dagger}}
are independent. By using
Eq. (429)
, we account for the 1
st
excited states and we therefore disregard the ground state and zero-point energy contribution to the structure factor which is expressed by the term
S^{\sf GS}_{\rm{O}}(\textbf{q}=0,\omega)
in
Eq. (428)
.

Finally, we find

S^{\sf QM}_{\rm{S}}(\textbf{q},\omega)=2\frac{\sqrt{A_{\textbf{q}}+B_{\textbf{q}}}}{\sqrt{A_{\textbf{q}}-B_{\textbf{q}}}}\delta(\omega-\omega_{\textbf{q}})+S^{\sf GS}_{\rm{S}}(\textbf{q}=0,\omega)\;,

(430)

where we used
Eq. (183)
.
Detailed calculations for
\textbf{q}=0
contributions to the dipole moment structure factor can be found in
Appendix
F.6
.
More precisely,
S^{\sf GS}_{\rm{S}}(\textbf{q}=0,\omega)
is given by
Eq. (468)
, which combined with
Eq. (430)
gives the total quantum structure factor for the dipole moments expressed in
Eq. (193)
.

F.3
Quadrupole moments: quantum structure factor at general values of
q

We now consider the dynamical structure factor associated with
quadrupole moments

S^{\sf QM}_{\rm{Q}}(\textbf{q},\omega)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}t}{2\pi}e^{i\omega t}\sum_{\mu\nu}{\langle{\hat{\rm{Q}}_{\textbf{q}}^{\mu\nu}}(t){\hat{\rm{Q}}_{-\textbf{q}}^{\mu\nu}}(0)\rangle}\;.

(431)

Following the same steps as for the spin–structure factor,
we use
Eq. (173)
to express the quadupole components up to linear order
in
Eq. (51)
. We find

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathrm{Q}}}}_{i}

\displaystyle\cong\begin{pmatrix}\frac{2}{3}&-{\hat{\rm{a}}_{i}^{\dagger}}-{\hat{\rm{a}}_{i}}&0\\
-{\hat{\rm{a}}_{i}^{\dagger}}-{\hat{\rm{a}}_{i}}&-\frac{4}{3}&-{\hat{\rm{b}}_{i}^{\dagger}}-{\hat{\rm{b}}_{i}}\\
0&-{\hat{\rm{b}}_{i}^{\dagger}}-{\hat{\rm{b}}_{i}}&\frac{2}{3}\end{pmatrix}\;,

(432)

An equivalent calculation of matrix elements in the Bogoliubov basis [
Eq. (182)
]
yields

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathrm{Q}}}}_{\textbf{q}}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\small{\begin{pmatrix}\frac{2}{3}\sqrt{N}\delta(\textbf{q})&\xi_{\sf{Q}}(\textbf{q})({\hat{\alpha}_{-\textbf{q}}^{\dagger}}+{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}}})&0\\
\xi_{\sf{Q}}(\textbf{q})({\hat{\alpha}_{-\textbf{q}}^{\dagger}}+{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}}})&-\frac{4}{3}\sqrt{N}\delta(\textbf{q})&\xi_{\sf{Q}}(\textbf{q})({\hat{\beta}_{-\textbf{q}}^{\dagger}}+{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{q}}})\\
0&\xi_{\sf{Q}}(\textbf{q})({\hat{\beta}_{-\textbf{q}}^{\dagger}}+{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{q}}})&\frac{2}{3}\sqrt{N}\delta(\textbf{q})\end{pmatrix}}\;,

(433)

where
N
is the number of sites and where
\xi_{\sf{Q}}(\textbf{q})
is the coherence factor for quadrupoles defined as

\xi_{\sf{Q}}(\textbf{q})=\frac{B_{\textbf{q}}-\Delta_{\textbf{q}}}{\sqrt{\Delta_{\textbf{q}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{q}}^{2}}}\;.

(434)

Using
Eq. (419)
, we can then calculate the structure factor for quadrupole moments as defined in
Eq. (431)
. We obtain

\displaystyle S^{\sf QM}_{\rm{Q}}(\textbf{q},\omega)=

\displaystyle 4\frac{\sqrt{A_{\textbf{q}}-B_{\textbf{q}}}}{\sqrt{A_{\textbf{q}}+B_{\textbf{q}}}}\delta(\omega-\omega_{\textbf{q}})+S^{\sf GS}_{\rm{Q}}(\textbf{q}=0,\omega)\;,

(435)

where we used
Eq. (183)
.
Detailed calculations for
\textbf{q}=0
contributions to the quadrupole moment structure factor can be found in
Appendix
F.7
.
More precisely,
S^{\sf GS}_{\rm{Q}}(\textbf{q}=0,\omega)
is given by
Eq. (479)
, which combined with
Eq. (435)
gives the total quantum structure factor for the quadrupole moments expressed in
Eq. (201)
.

F.4
A-matrices : quantum structure factors at general values of
q

The most fundamental objects in our theory are not dipoles or quadrupoles,
but the A–matrices which describe the quantum state of the
spin–1
moment.
It is therefore useful to introduce a dynamical structure factor

S^{\sf QM}_{\rm{A}}(\textbf{q},\omega)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}t}{2\pi}e^{i\omega t}\sum_{\mu\nu}{\langle{\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{\penalty\ \nu}^{\mu}}(t){\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{\penalty\ \mu}^{\nu}}(0)\rangle}\;.

(436)

Neglecting 2
nd
order and higher terms,
Eq. (173)
becomes

{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathrm{A}}}}_{i}\simeq\begin{pmatrix}0&{\hat{\rm{a}}_{i}^{\dagger}}&0\\
{\hat{\rm{a}}_{i}}&1&{\hat{\rm{b}}_{i}}\\
0&{\hat{\rm{b}}_{i}^{\dagger}}&0\end{pmatrix}\;.

(437)

Once again we can use the Bogoliubov basis [
Eq. (182)
] to find

{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathrm{A}}}}_{\textbf{q}}\simeq\begin{pmatrix}\begin{array}[]{c c c c c c}\lx@intercol\hfil\hbox{\multirowsetup 0}\hfil\lx@intercol&\hbox{\multirowsetup{\small$\xi_{\sf{A}}^{-}(\textbf{q}){\hat{\alpha}_{-\textbf{q}}^{\dagger}}$}}&\lx@intercol\hfil\hbox{\multirowsetup 0}\hfil\lx@intercol\\
&&\hbox{\multirowsetup{\small$-\xi_{\sf{A}}^{+}(\textbf{q}){\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}}}$}}&&\\
\hbox{\multirowsetup{\small$-\xi_{\sf{A}}^{+}(\textbf{q}){\hat{\alpha}_{-\textbf{q}}^{\dagger}}$ }}&\lx@intercol\hfil\hbox{\multirowsetup$\sqrt{N}\delta_{\textbf{q},0}$ }\hfil\lx@intercol&\hbox{\multirowsetup{\small$-\xi_{\sf{A}}^{+}(\textbf{q}){\hat{\beta}_{-\textbf{q}}^{\dagger}}$}}\\
\hbox{\multirowsetup{\small$+\xi_{\sf{A}}^{-}(\textbf{q}){\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}}}$}}&&&\hbox{\multirowsetup{\small$+\xi_{\sf{A}}^{-}(\textbf{q}){\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{q}}}$}}\\
\lx@intercol\hfil\hbox{\multirowsetup 0}\hfil\lx@intercol&\hbox{\multirowsetup{\small$\xi_{\sf{A}}^{-}(\textbf{q}){\hat{\beta}_{-\textbf{q}}^{\dagger}}$}}&\lx@intercol\hfil\hbox{\multirowsetup 0}\hfil\lx@intercol\\
&&\hbox{\multirowsetup{\small$-\xi_{\sf{A}}^{+}(\textbf{q}){\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{q}}}$}}&&\end{array}\end{pmatrix}

(438)

where
N
is the number of sites and
\xi_{\sf{A}}^{+}(\textbf{q})
and
\xi_{\sf{A}}^{-}(\textbf{q})
are the coherence factors for A–matrices defined as

\displaystyle\xi_{\sf{A}}^{+}(\textbf{q})=\frac{\xi_{\sf{S}}(\textbf{q})+\xi_{\sf{Q}}(\textbf{q})}{2}\;,

(439a)

\displaystyle\xi_{\sf{A}}^{-}(\textbf{q})=\frac{\xi_{\sf{S}}(\textbf{q})-\xi_{\sf{Q}}(\textbf{q})}{2}\;,

(439b)

where
\xi_{\sf{S}}(\textbf{q})
and
\xi_{\sf{Q}}(\textbf{q})
are defined in
Eq. (427)
and
Eq. (434)
respectively.

Using
Eq. (419)
, we can then calculate the structure factor for quadrupole moments as defined in
Eq. (436)
. We obtain

\displaystyle S_{\rm{A}}^{\sf QM}(\textbf{q},\omega)=

\displaystyle 2\frac{A_{\textbf{q}}}{\sqrt{A_{\textbf{q}}^{2}-B_{\textbf{q}}^{2}}}\delta(\omega-\omega_{\textbf{q}})+S^{\sf GS}_{\rm{A}}(\textbf{q}=0,\omega)\;,

(440)

where we used
Eq. (183)
.
Detailed calculations for
\textbf{q}=0
contributions to the A–matrix structure factor can be found in
Appendix
F.8
.
More precisely,
S^{\sf GS}_{\rm{A}}(\textbf{q}=0,\omega)
is given by
, which combined with
Eq. (440)
gives the total quantum structure factor for the A-matrices expressed in
Eq. (208)
.

F.5
Quantum structure factors: contribution of the ground state at
\textbf{q}=0

We here present how to describe the zero-temperature correction of the ground state and the zero-point energy fluctuations’ contribution to the quantum structure factors, which is expected to happen at
\textbf{q}=0
and
\omega=0
.

We therefore consider the zero–temperature quantum structure factor at
\textbf{q}=0
to be given by

S^{\sf GS}_{\rm{O}}(\textbf{q}=0)=\sum_{\alpha\beta}{\langle\hat{O}^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}=0,\beta}\hat{O}^{\beta}_{\textbf{q}=0,\alpha}\rangle}_{T=0}\;.

(441)

Calculating the contribution of the ground state and the zero-point energy fluctuations to the zero–temperature quantum structure factor can be achieved by adding a source term to the BBQ Hamiltonian that includes a fictive field
\bf{h}
coupled to the spin moments, similarly to what we did for the classical case [see
Section IV.3.2
and
Appendix
D
]. The structure factors can then be calculated by taking the appropriate derivative of the free energy with respect to the fictive field
\bf{h}
.

We consider the total Hamiltonian to be given by
Eq. (153)
, and the source term to be of the form given in
Eq. (154)
.
We can then rewrite the operators
\hat{O}^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}\beta}
of
Eq. (154)
in function of the fluctuations orthogonal to the FQ ground state [
Eq. (112)
]. Refer to
Section IV.1
for details on the creation of orthogonal fluctuations.
Expanding the source term Hamiltonian [
Eq. (154)
] up to second order in bosons, Fourier transforming it and considering its contribution for
\textbf{q}=0
, we can assume that it takes the following form

\displaystyle\Delta{\mathcal{H}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}}]=

\displaystyle C[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\textbf{k}}\left[{\boldsymbol{\hat{\rm{w}}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}}{m}_{\textbf{k}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]{\boldsymbol{\hat{\rm{w}}}_{\textbf{k}}}\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\left[{\textbf{N}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]^{T}{\boldsymbol{\hat{\rm{w}}}_{\textbf{k}}}+{\boldsymbol{\hat{\rm{w}}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}}{\textbf{N}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]\right]\delta_{\textbf{k},0}\right]\;,

(442)

where
C[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]
is the coefficient for the 0
th
order term of the source term expended in terms of the fluctuations orthogonal to the FQ ground state,
{\boldsymbol{\hat{\rm{w}}}_{\textbf{k}}}
represents these fluctuations orthogonal to the FQ ground state and is given
Eq. (175)
,
{m}_{\textbf{k}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]
represents the interaction matrix for 2
nd
order terms in fluctuations and depends on
{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}
, and where
{\textbf{N}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]^{\dagger}
and
{\textbf{N}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]
are the coefficients for the linear terms in fluctuations. By definition of the source term Hamiltonian [
Eq. (154)
], all the coefficients
C[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]
,
{\textbf{N}}_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]^{T}
,
{\textbf{N}}_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]
, and
{m}_{\textbf{k}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]
depend linearly on the fictive field
\bf{h}
and will be different whether we are considering dipole, quadrupole, or A-matrix moments for the source term [
Eq. (154)
].

Using
Eq. (174)
for the BBQ Hamiltonian, we can assume that the total Hamiltonian [
Eq. (153)
] in terms of the bosons then take the following form

\displaystyle{\cal H}=

\displaystyle E_{0}+C[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\textbf{k}}\left[{\boldsymbol{\hat{\rm{w}}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}}{M}_{\textbf{k}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]{\boldsymbol{\hat{\rm{w}}}_{\textbf{k}}}\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\left[{\textbf{N}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]^{\dagger}{\boldsymbol{\hat{\rm{w}}}_{\textbf{k}}}+{\boldsymbol{\hat{\rm{w}}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}}{\textbf{N}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]\right]\delta_{\textbf{k},0}\right]\;,

(443)

where
E_{0}
is the mean-field ground–state given in
Eq. (125)
, and where
{M}_{\textbf{k}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]
is the interaction matrix for the total Hamiltonian. It includes contributions from the BBQ Hamiltonian and the source term
{m}_{\textbf{k}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]
, and, therefore, depends on
{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}
.

Following the method described in
Appendix
E
, we perform a Bogoliubov transformation in order to diagonalize the total Hamiltonian. We assume that the new Bogoliubov bosons
{\boldsymbol{\hat{\rm{v}}}_{\textbf{k}}}

{\boldsymbol{\hat{\rm{v}}}_{\textbf{k}}}=\begin{pmatrix}{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}}\\
{\hat{\alpha}_{-\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}\\
{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}}}\\
{\hat{\beta}_{-\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}\\
\end{pmatrix}\;,

(444)

are given in terms of the bosons orthogonal to the FQ ground state
{\boldsymbol{\hat{\rm{w}}}_{\textbf{k}}}
by
Eq. (381)
.
We can then assume that the total Hamiltonian in terms of the Bogoliubov bosons
{\boldsymbol{\hat{\rm{v}}}_{\textbf{k}}}
becomes

\displaystyle{\cal H}=

\displaystyle E_{0}+\Delta E_{0}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]+C[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\textbf{k}}\left[\epsilon_{\textbf{k},\alpha}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}}+\epsilon_{\textbf{k},\beta}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}}}\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\left[\tilde{\textbf{N}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]^{\dagger}{\boldsymbol{\hat{\rm{v}}}_{\textbf{k}}}+{\boldsymbol{\hat{\rm{v}}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}}\tilde{\textbf{N}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]\right]\delta_{\textbf{k},0}\right]\;,

(445)

where
\epsilon_{\textbf{k},\alpha}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]
and
\epsilon_{\textbf{k},\beta}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]
are the two physical eigenvalues obtained by diagonalizing
{M}_{\textbf{k}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]
[see
Eq. (385)
], where
\Delta E_{0}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]
is the ground state contribution of the Bogoluibov bosons and where

\begin{matrix}\tilde{\textbf{N}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]^{\dagger}={\textbf{N}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]^{\dagger}U^{-1}\;,\\
\tilde{\textbf{N}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]={U^{\dagger}}^{-1}{\textbf{N}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]\;.\end{matrix}

(446)

Here,
U
is the Bogoliubov matrix change defined by
Eq. (381)
and remains to be determined.
We also note that
U^{-1}
(and
{U^{\dagger}}^{-1}
) should be calculate from
Eq. (382)
.

The canonical partition function is defined by

Z=Tr(e^{-\beta\hat{{\cal H}}})\;,

(447)

where
\beta
is defined by
Eq. (134)
, and
\hat{{\cal H}}
is the operator Hamiltonian.
However in order to compute the partition function and the free energy, we want to get rid of the linear terms
\tilde{\textbf{N}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]^{\dagger}
and
\tilde{\textbf{N}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]
, which only contribute for
\textbf{k}=0
.
The partition function is then the one of a set of independent harmonic oscillators for the
\textbf{k}\neq 0
terms, but still contains linear terms with respect to the bosons for
\textbf{k}=0
:

\displaystyle Z=

\displaystyle Tr\left(e^{-\beta E_{0}-\beta\Delta E_{0}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]-\beta C[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]}\right.

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\textbf{k}\neq 0}(e^{-\beta\epsilon_{\textbf{k},\alpha}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}}})(e^{\beta\epsilon_{\textbf{k},\beta}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}}}})

\displaystyle\times(e^{-\frac{1}{2}\beta\epsilon_{\textbf{k}=0,\alpha}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}=0}^{\dagger}}{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}=0}}-\frac{1}{2}\beta n_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}=0}^{\dagger}}-\frac{1}{2}\beta n_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}=0}}})

\displaystyle\left.\times(e^{-\frac{1}{2}\beta\epsilon_{\textbf{k}=0,\beta}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}=0}^{\dagger}}{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}=0}}-\frac{1}{2}\beta n_{3}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}=0}^{\dagger}}-\frac{1}{2}\beta n_{4}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}=0}}})\right)\;.

(448)

where

\begin{matrix}n_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]=\tilde{N}[{\bf{h}}_{-\textbf{k}=0}]^{\dagger,2}+\tilde{N}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]^{1}\;,\\
n_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]=\tilde{N}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]^{\dagger,1}+\tilde{N}[{\bf{h}}_{-\textbf{k}=0}]^{2}\;,\\
n_{3}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]=\tilde{N}[{\bf{h}}_{-\textbf{k}=0}]^{\dagger,4}+\tilde{N}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]^{3}\;,\\
n_{4}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]=\tilde{N}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]^{\dagger,3}+\tilde{N}[{\bf{h}}_{-\textbf{k}=0}]^{4}\;,\end{matrix}

(449)

with
\tilde{\textbf{N}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]^{\dagger,1}
denoting the first component of
\tilde{\textbf{N}}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}}]^{\dagger}
.
To do this, we note that we can perform a change a variables by completing the square. For the
\textbf{k}=0
term, for the the
{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}},{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}}
bosons for instance, we have

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\beta\epsilon_{0,\alpha}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]{\hat{\alpha}_{0}^{\dagger}}{\hat{\alpha}_{0}}-\frac{1}{2}\beta n_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]{\hat{\alpha}_{0}^{\dagger}}-\frac{1}{2}\beta n_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]{\hat{\alpha}_{0}}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\beta\epsilon_{0,\alpha}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]({\hat{\alpha}_{0}^{\dagger}}+\frac{n_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]}{\epsilon_{0,\alpha}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]})({\hat{\alpha}_{0}}+\frac{n_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]}{\epsilon_{0,\alpha}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]})

\displaystyle+\beta\frac{n_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]n_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]}{\epsilon_{0,\alpha}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]}\;,

(450)

We note that we have

n_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]=n_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]^{\dagger}\;,

(451)

so that we can define the change of variable

\begin{matrix}{\hat{\rho}_{\textbf{k}=0}^{\dagger}}={\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}=0}^{\dagger}}+\frac{n_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]}{\epsilon_{\textbf{k}=0,\alpha}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]}\;,\\
{\hat{\rho}_{\textbf{k}=0}}={\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}=0}}+\frac{n_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]}{\epsilon_{\textbf{k}=0,\alpha}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]}\;,\end{matrix}

(452)

which ensures that
{\hat{\rho}_{\textbf{k}=0}^{\dagger}}
and
{\hat{\rho}_{\textbf{k}=0}}
have bosonic commutation relations and are associated with the eigenmode
\epsilon_{\textbf{k}=0,\alpha}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]
.
We follow the same argument for the
{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}=0}^{\dagger}},{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}=0}}
bosons, and get new bosons
{\hat{\sigma}_{\textbf{k}=0}^{\dagger}}
and
{\hat{\sigma}_{\textbf{k}=0}}
:

\begin{matrix}{\hat{\sigma}_{\textbf{k}=0}^{\dagger}}={\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}=0}^{\dagger}}+\frac{n_{3}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]}{\epsilon_{\textbf{k}=0,\beta}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]}\;,\\
{\hat{\sigma}_{\textbf{k}=0}}={\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}=0}}+\frac{n_{4}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]}{\epsilon_{\textbf{k}=0,\beta}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]}\;,\end{matrix}

(453)

associated with the eigenmode
\epsilon_{\textbf{k}=0,\beta}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]

The partition function is then the one of a set of independent harmonic oscillators. We obtain

\displaystyle Z=

\displaystyle Tr\left(e^{-\beta E_{0}-\beta\Delta E_{0}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]-\beta C[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]}\right.

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\textbf{k}\neq 0}(e^{-\beta\epsilon_{\textbf{k},\alpha}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}}})(e^{-\beta\epsilon_{\textbf{k},\beta}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}}}})

\displaystyle\times(e^{-\beta\epsilon_{\textbf{k}=0,\alpha}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]{\hat{\rho}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{\hat{\rho}_{\textbf{k}}}})(e^{-\beta\epsilon_{\textbf{k}=0,\beta}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]{\hat{\sigma}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}{\hat{\sigma}_{\textbf{k}}}})

\displaystyle\left.\times e^{\beta\frac{n_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]n_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]}{\epsilon_{\textbf{k}=0,\alpha}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]}}e^{\beta\frac{n_{3}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]n_{4}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]}{\epsilon_{\textbf{k}=0,\alpha}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]}}\right)\;.

(454)

We then perform the trace on the Fock space, and use the fact that the trace is independent of the choice of the basis. This means we can compute it separately for the
{\hat{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}
bosons on their respective Fock basis
{\left|n^{\alpha}_{\textbf{k}}\right\rangle}
and for the
{\hat{\rho}_{\textbf{k}=0}^{\dagger}}
bosons on its respective Fock basis
{\left|n^{\rho}_{\textbf{k}=0}\right\rangle}
, and similarly for
{\hat{\beta}_{\textbf{k}}^{\dagger}}
and
{\hat{\sigma}_{\textbf{k}=0}^{\dagger}}
.
Taking the trace over the Fock space as explained above, we obtain

\displaystyle Z=

\displaystyle e^{-\beta E_{0}-\beta\Delta E_{0}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]-\beta C[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\textbf{k}}\frac{1}{1-e^{-\beta\epsilon_{\textbf{k},\alpha}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]}}\frac{1}{1-e^{-\beta\epsilon_{\textbf{k},\alpha}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]}}

\displaystyle\times e^{\beta\frac{n_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]n_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]}{\epsilon_{\textbf{k},\alpha}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]}}e^{\beta\frac{n_{3}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]n_{4}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]}{\epsilon_{\textbf{k},\alpha}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]}}\;.

(455)

We note here that instead of doing the change of variable for
\textbf{k}=0
, we could also
The free energy is given by

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle=-\frac{\log(Z)}{\beta}

\displaystyle=E_{0}+\Delta E_{0}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]+C[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\beta}\sum_{\textbf{k}}\log(1-e^{-\beta\epsilon_{\textbf{k},\alpha}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\beta}\sum_{\textbf{k}}\log(1-e^{-\beta\epsilon_{\textbf{k},\beta}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]})

\displaystyle-2\frac{n_{1}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]n_{2}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]}{\epsilon_{\textbf{k},\alpha}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]}-2\frac{n_{3}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]n_{4}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{k}=0}]}{\epsilon_{\textbf{k},\alpha}[{\bf{h}}_{\textbf{q}=0}]}+{\mathcal{O}}(T^{2})\;.

(456)

The moments are given by taking the appropriate derivative of the free energy. They are given by the same expression that we obtained for the classical case expressed in
Eq. (323)
and
Eq. (324)
.
We now note that we are interested in the zero temperature
T=0
structure factor, and we can disregard the terms with
\frac{1}{\beta}
in the free energy.

Eq. (323)
then becomes

\displaystyle{\langle\hat{O}^{\alpha}_{\textbf{q}=0,\beta}\rangle}_{T=0}
